Sandstorm Golduck — Psyduck Step flips a coin once per turn while Golduck is Active and free of Special Conditions; on heads, the opposing Active Pokémon becomes Confused. PvE/PvP supported.
Holon Phantoms Vileplume — Poison Pollen flips a coin once per turn while Vileplume is free of Special Conditions; on heads, the opposing Active Pokémon becomes Poisoned. PvE/PvP supported.
Legend Collection Gengar — moves one damage counter between two opposing Pokémon once per turn while free of Special Conditions, including a Knock Out. PvE/PvP supported.
Unified Minds Jynx — Ominous Posture moves one damage counter between two of its controller’s Pokémon once per turn. PvE/PvP supported.
Generations Meowstic-EX — Shadow Ear moves one damage counter from one of its controller’s Pokémon to an opposing Pokémon once per turn while Active. PvE/PvP supported.
Sandstorm Xatu — once per turn, heals one damage counter from each of its controller’s Active Pokémon while free of Special Conditions. PvE/PvP supported.
Great Encounters Wigglytuff — Good Night Melody makes both players’ Active Pokémon Asleep once per turn while Wigglytuff is free of Special Conditions. PvE/PvP supported.
Holon Phantoms Mew ex — Psychic Vision inspects the opponent’s hand once per turn from the Bench. PvE/PvP supported.
Skyridge Snorlax — once per turn while Active and free of Special Conditions, heal one damage counter from itself, then become Asleep. PvE/PvP supported.
Silver Tempest Radiant Alakazam — Painful Spoons moves up to two damage counters from one opposing Pokémon to another once per turn. PvE/PvP supported.
Legend Maker Banette ex / Dragon Frontiers Typhlosion δ / Great Encounters Cresselia LV.X — once per turn, move one damage counter between any two Pokémon on either side; Banette ex and Typhlosion δ must be Active, all are blocked by Special Conditions. PvE/PvP supported.
Paldea Evolved Shinx — once per turn while Active, switch the opponent’s Active Pokémon to the Bench; the opponent chooses the replacement. PvE/PvP supported.
Deoxys Ninjask — Fast Protection prevents all attack effects, including damage, from opposing Basic Pokémon. PvE/PvP supported.
POP Series 7 Wormadam Sandy Cloak — Sandy Cloak prevents attack effects, excluding damage, to Wormadam Sandy Cloak. PvE/PvP supported.
Delta Species Volbeat/Illumise — partner-dependent attack protection: Illumise blocks all effects and damage to Volbeat from Pokémon-ex attacks; Volbeat blocks all effects and damage to Illumise from Dark-named Pokémon attacks. PvE/PvP supported.
Legend Maker Latias δ/Latios δ — Dual Aura suppresses Poké-Bodies on evolved non-Pokémon-ex on both sides while the matching Latias/Latios partner is in play. PvE/PvP supported.
Legend Maker Wobbuffet — while evolved, adds one Colorless to the opposing Active Pokémon’s Retreat Cost. PvE/PvP supported.
Energy-conditioned no-Weakness rules use the shared Energy count and selected Energy type as the current format’s approximation for individual attached Energy; Special Energy, Tool, Stadium, Prize, and delayed-turn conditions remain unsupported.
Passive no-Weakness effects are supported for your board, your Active Pokémon, selected Pokémon types, and Fire/Metal Pokémon across both sides; Kingdra can protect your Active Pokémon while it remains in play. Stadium, Tool, attached-Energy, and delayed-turn conditions remain unsupported here.
Dragon Frontiers Electabuzz δ — once per turn, while evolved and free of Special Conditions, draw the bottom card of your deck. PvE/PvP supported.
Deoxys Claydol — Cosmic Power shuffles your hand into your deck, then draws up to the opponent's hand size once per turn; requires Claydol Active and free of Special Conditions. PvE/PvP supported.
White Flare and SV Black Star Promos Gothitelle — Active-only, once per turn, opponent shuffles hand into deck and draws up to 3. PvE/PvP supported.
Diamond & Pearl Unown [D] — once per turn from the Bench, flip a coin; on heads, both players draw one card, with the player's draw first. PvE and PvP supported.
Unbroken Bonds Salazzle and Ninetales — pay one Fire Energy from the shared pool to draw 3 cards once per turn; Ninetales also retains its Special Condition restriction. Player and PvP actions validate the selected pool type and Energy count.
Astral Radiance Radiant Greninja — Concealed Cards pays one Energy from the shared pool to draw 2 cards once per turn. Player and PvP actions validate and spend the pool unit instead of requiring a hand Energy card.
Lost Origin Drapion VSTAR — Venomous Vortex applies Paralysis and Poison to a selected opposing Active Pokémon and raises its Poison checkup damage to 30; it uses the shared once-per-game VSTAR Power limit in PvE and PvP.
Trainer-card searches are not supported by the Pokémon-only battle deck. Supporter, Item, Stadium, and Pokémon Tool searches remain unmatched in the Ability audit and are not offered as usable battle Abilities.

# Battle Mechanics Inventory

Secret Wonders Unown [Z] can use ZERO from the Bench once per turn when its deck is empty, putting Unown Z on top of the deck in PvE and PvP.

Deoxys Jirachi's Wish Maker picks one of the top five cards, shuffles the rest back into the deck, and makes all living friendly Active Pokémon Asleep. Jirachi must be Active and free of Special Conditions in PvE and PvP.

Deoxys Sableye can inspect the opponent's hand once per turn while Active and free of Special Conditions in PvE and PvP.

Diamond & Pearl Unown [C] flips once per turn while on the Bench; heads searches a Basic Unown from the deck to the Bench. PvE and PvP are supported.

Diamond & Pearl Unown [A] reorders the top two cards of its owner's deck once per turn while on the Bench in PvE and PvP.

BW Black Star Promos Mew's Psyscan reveals the opponent's hand once per turn while Mew is Active, in PvE and PvP.

Astral Radiance/Crown Zenith Darkrai VSTAR — recover up to two Item cards from discard to hand once per game using the shared VSTAR Power limit, with Ability suppression and discard membership checked in PvE and PvP.

151/Paldean Fates Snorlax — Voraciousness recovers up to two available Leftovers cards from discard to hand once per turn, with Ability suppression and discard membership checked in PvE and PvP.

Rebel Clash Eldegoss V — on Bench entry from hand, return a chosen Supporter from discard to hand. PvE and PvP validate that the selected card is a Supporter and check Ability suppression.

Rising Rivals Starmie adds one Water Energy to the shared pool once per turn as the engine's approximation for returning a Basic Water Energy card from discard to hand. Player, PvP, and NPC paths enforce its Special Condition restriction, Ability suppression, pool cap, and usage limit.

Supreme Victors Solrock can rearrange the top three cards of its controller's deck once per turn when Lunatone is in play. Its Power is unavailable under Special Conditions.

Supreme Victors Garchomp C LV.X can heal all damage from its owner's Pokémon SP with Healing Breath when played onto Active Garchomp C. Healing prevention and Ability suppression disable it.

Arceus Lopunny's Hustle Step discards an Energy card from hand to heal 10 damage from each of its owner's Pokémon once per turn. Special Conditions and healing prevention disable it.

Arceus Gengar can move one damage counter between opposing Pokémon once per turn. Its Special Condition restriction and damage-counter movement prevention are enforced.

Arceus Salamence's Top Entry reveals the top card of its deck once per turn, adding Basic Energy to the shared Energy pool and discarding other cards. Special Conditions prevent use.

Arceus Gengar LV.X can remove an opposing Pokémon LV.X and shuffle that card into its owner's deck once per turn. The Pokémon beneath is restored, with existing damage and status preserved; Special Conditions and Ability suppression disable the Power.

Platinum Drapion LV.X's Poké-Power flips once per turn to Poison an opposing Active Pokémon; heads sets its Poison checkup to 30 damage. Target selection, Special Condition limits, Ability suppression, and NPC use are supported.

Stormfront Raichu LV.X's Link Lightning grants one additional attack after Voltage Shoot. Player, PvP, and NPC attack flows enforce the named-attack trigger, same-attacker follow-up, Special Conditions, and Ability suppression.

Legends Awakened Unown J's Bench-entry Power flips once and returns a chosen Trainer from the discard pile to hand on heads. Player, PvP, and NPC paths track the flip, once-per-turn use, and Ability suppression.

Majestic Dawn Garchomp LV.X's Dragon Pulse flips three coins when played from hand onto Active Garchomp. Each heads places one damage counter on every opposing Benched Pokémon; the coin results are recorded.

Great Encounters Unown H's HEAL removes all Special Conditions from one friendly Active Pokémon once per turn when Unown H, E, A, and L are on the Bench. Player, PvP, and NPC use respect Ability suppression.

- Unreleased: Rock Tunnel's Hidden Chamber now uses Underground Mining, with an independent 0.1% chance to bury a Metal Coat evolution item in each wall. The existing 12% Rusty Coat held-item recipe unlock remains a reward for clearing the wall. Fully uncovered Metal Coats are retained on collapse under this encounter's `complete: false` policy.

Release `0.51.20` removes manual battle effects and enemy-use rules from berries. Eligible healing/status berries act only through their existing held-item triggers and ability interactions; battle inventories and singles/doubles item actions reject loose berries. The Squirtle Squad Chronicle keeps its held berries and no longer supplies loose battle berries.

Release `0.51.12` sets shared base Alpha odds to 1-in-55 for wild battles, location captures, fishing, and arcade-authority encounters, retaining eligibility rules and keep-net modifiers. The Beast Below is a regular Steelix; its stats, moves, level cap, and Metal Coat reward are unchanged.

Double-battle update: Sketch now captures from the selected opposing lane rather than assuming a single active opponent. The existing Research Level chance, eligible TM-backed move filtering, and post-win Sketchbook persistence remain unchanged.

Release `0.33.0` adds `rarityChances` to `BattleConfig`, `BattleEnemy`, `Location`, and `LocationEncounter`, using all registered special rarities. Thresholds are 0–1: omissions inherit and explicit zero disables. Capture/wild shiny defaults to 1/512; trainer battles default all thresholds to zero. Each generated enemy rolls independently: the smallest qualifying threshold wins, equal thresholds break ties uniformly, and normal occupies the remainder. Fixed rarity and explicit shiny/shadow/radiant flags take precedence. Selected rarity is persisted before applying existing entry effects, including Shadow and Radiant behavior. Player, copied rival, and scripted teams retain configured rarities. Events can temporarily modify standard battle teams, moves, items, limits, rewards, XP, and rarity thresholds without introducing new move or ability definitions.

Last reviewed: 2026-09-15

Release `0.36.0` adds doubles move targeting (`opponent`, `ally`, `self`, `any-single`, `both-opponents`, `both-allies`, `all-active`) and priority metadata to authored TM moves. The previously withheld doubles-only TMs are Helping Hand (Plusle Research 2), Follow Me (Clefairy Research 3), Rage Powder (Butterfree Research 3), Ally Switch (Ralts Research 4), and After You (Torkoal Research 3); normal form compatibility and assignment rules still apply, and singles rejects their use. Existing spread, partner-healing, side-field, guard, and standard priority moves receive explicit doubles overrides in `src/data/moves/tms/doubles.ts`. Prankster, Triage, Gale Wings, Quick Draw, Stall, and Mycelium Might adjust doubles ordering. The new Kanto/Test four-Pokémon trial is reward-free; separate friendly and ranked doubles PvP entries retain their respective single-format reward rules. Partner battle-ability hooks are active only when two Pokémon share a side: Battery, Power Spot, Friend Guard, Plus/Minus, Steely Spirit, Telepathy, Victory Star, Dazzling/Armor Tail/Queenly Majesty, Hospitality, Costar, Commander, Symbiosis, Flower Veil, Sweet Veil, Healer, Receiver, and Power of Alchemy. Historical table rows describing these as inert refer to singles; doubles uses the new partner hooks. Pressure's extra move-use depletion applies to each targeted holder.

Move-based evolution now checks the saved assigned move, not a Charged TM. Required moves are Rollout (Lickitung), Ancient Power (Tangela, Yanma, Piloswine), Double Hit (Aipom), Twin Beam (Girafarig), Hyper Drill (Dunsparce), Barb Barrage (Hisuian Qwilfish), Mimic (Bonsly, Mime Jr.), Stomp (Steenee), Dragon Pulse (Poipole), Taunt (Clobbopus), and Dragon Cheer (Dipplin). All are already compatible with their source forms and use normal TM ownership and assignment unlocks. Evolution does not consume the move or its TM.

Release 0.26.0 introduces a shared structured move presentation used by MoveDex/Sketchbook, TM and HM inventory notes, Pokédex compatibility notes, Pokémon loadouts, and battle decisions. It distinguishes base power from special fixed/OHKO/delayed models, exposes always-hit behavior, targets, chances, durations, scopes, use conditions, charge/recharge, multi-hit behavior, dynamic type rules, and costs. Move descriptions are no longer runtime inputs for healing or recoil classification. Self damage now authors an explicit `on-hit`, `on-miss`, or `on-use` trigger, and Strength Sap authors target-Attack healing. The corrected catalog entries include Nuzzle, Geomancy, Heal Bell, Jungle Healing, Lunar Blessing, Draining Kiss, Synthesis, Moonlight, Strength Sap, Shell Side Arm, Fiery Wrath, High Jump Kick, Jump Kick, and Axe Kick.

Sketch resolves target availability before its Research Level-scaled capture chance (25% at Level 1, 30% at Level 2, 35% at Level 3, 40% at Level 4, and 50% at Level 5) in both PvE and PvP. Targets with no eligible TM-backed move and targets whose eligible moves are all already recorded produce their specific unavailable message without consuming the capture roll; only a non-empty pool of new moves can produce capture success or random failure.

Release 0.14.0 adds Koga's original `swift-poison` Gym TM. It is a 90%-accuracy Poison Speed move that deals 0.2x damage, poisons on a successful hit, succeeds only when the user's effective Speed is higher than the target's, and prevents the target's same-turn counterattack when successful.

Release 0.13.0 adds no new move or ability definitions. The rebuilt Gym Leader Chronicles now assign canonical existing moves per enemy and per-activity player loadout, use `trainer` through `boss` AI profiles, and lower attack-telegraph rates by anthology progression from Brock (70%) to Giovanni (20%).

This document tracks authored Pokemon abilities, battle moves, TMs, field TMs, and power systems. Keep it in sync with:

- `src/data/abilities.ts` and generated ability helpers under `src/data/abilities/`
- `src/data/items/entries/ability-patches.ts`
- `src/data/moves/tms/*.ts`
- `src/data/moves/entries/status-effects.ts`
- `src/data/items/entries/tms.ts`
- `src/data/items/entries/key-items.ts`
- `src/data/items/entries/battle.ts`
- `src/data/items/entries/berries.ts`
- `src/data/powers.ts`
- content rewards/requirements in `src/data/**/entries`

## Abilities

Abilities are assigned to caught Pokemon from `src/data/abilities.ts`. Main-game ability definitions are generated into `src/data/abilities/canonical-generated.ts` from the local PokeAPI CSV cache by `scripts/generate-canonical-abilities.mjs`; the active registry is currently canonical-only. The exported lookup map is derived from those generated canonical records. Runtime behavior resolves through normalized `AbilityEffect` data from `src/data/abilities/effects.ts`, with exact authored battle effects where supported and conservative best-effort battle approximations for every other canonical ability. Assignment comes from three pools:

- Global pool: currently empty.
- Type pool: currently empty.
- Form pool: `ABILITY_FORM_MAP` is built from ability metadata at load time. Canonical abilities use `naturalAssignments`, keyed by form ID, with normal slots resolving to a guaranteed base ability and hidden ability slots rolling first at 5%.

When a Pokemon evolves, it preserves the source Pokemon's natural ability slot on the evolved form. Matching target slots are used when available; otherwise, the evolved form uses the first target assignment with the same hidden/normal visibility before falling back to a natural target-form roll.

Canonical natural abilities now cover all generated Pokemon forms with source-data ability slots. Supported battle effects include active-Pokemon type immunities, type absorbs with healing/stat boosts, status immunities, Pokemon-type-gated status and stat-stage drop immunities, mental effect immunities, opponent-caused stat-drop immunities, boost triggers, stat-stage reflection, main-status reflection, main-status counter steps, opponent boost copying, entry ability copying, entry opponent move and held-item scouting, entry fainted-ally ability copying, display-only identity masking, entry transforms, attacker bypass for defender-side battle ability protections, switch prevention and immunity, forced-switch immunity, switch-out healing/status cure triggers, low-HP self-switch triggers, outgoing damage-reduction immunity for accuracy-style statuses, on-damaged stat-stage triggers, on-damaged stance-disable triggers, on-damaged status/secondary-status/chip/KO-damage/side-status/ability-change/type-change triggers, post-damage attacker status riders, recoil self-damage prevention and recoil-move damage boosts, indirect-damage immunity for status ticks/weather chip/hazards/secondary-status residual damage, weather-gated end-turn healing/damage triggers, end-turn status cure triggers, entry weather setting with primal-weather replacement blocking, weather-driven active battle type changes, before-attack active battle type changes, terrain-driven active battle type changes, held-item-driven active battle type changes, entry terrain setting, on-damaged terrain setting, terrain damage modifiers and grounded terrain status/priority/healing sub-rules, weather residual-damage immunity, incoming and field-scope move-ID blocking, incoming non-damaging status-move blocking/bounce logs, added-effect suppression on damaging moves, added-effect chance multiplication, added-effect damage boosts, ability-granted same-turn interruption chance from damaging moves, move accuracy multipliers/caps/bypass, ability-granted priority move contests, move-last counter-prevention suppression, move-ID-gated outgoing damage boosts, extra-hit damage events, contact-like reactive effect immunity, held-item protection against opposing removal/theft/swaps/berry consumption, held-item effect suppression, on-damaged and post-damage held-item theft, held-berry trigger thresholds/effect multipliers, post-consumption healing, consumed-berry restoration, held-item-loss-gated stat multipliers, before-move skips, self move-lock, full-HP survival, opposing move-use depletion, active-turn-limited stat multipliers, interruption immunities, critical-hit immunities/guarantees/modifiers, after-KO stat-stage triggers, stat multipliers, damage multipliers, Wonder Guard, Disguise/Ice Face shields, and form-change abilities including entry and Tera-activation field hooks. Every canonical ability resolves to at least one battle effect; unsupported cartridge mechanics such as doubles redirection, true contact-only filtering, full cartridge grounded overrides from items/field effects, broad ability copying/masking, and move-flag-specific boosts are approximated with thematic effects supported by the current single-battle engine. `docs/game-data/ability-implementation-audit.md` tracks known bad approximations and remaining engine gaps. A curated set of canonical abilities also has conservative secondary Capture and Field Observation effects. These are additive to battle behavior and include small catch-rate/timer assists, success-only item or research XP rewards, Field Observation pool weighting, spawn visibility/count modifiers, global Pokemon event odds multipliers, extra collectibles, collectible duration/quantity boosts, reward protection, and small Field Observation research XP boosts. No current canonical secondary effect changes shiny odds, replaces encounters, changes encounter level, or fails encounters. Battle ability effects are active-Pokemon only: partner/companion abilities do not apply to a Pokemon that is not currently fighting.

AbilityDex registration is stored in `user-abilitydex-entries`. Wild catches register the rolled ability after the Pokemon is created. Pokemon granted through the reward system use explicit `pokemonData.ability` as an override, otherwise roll the rewarded form's canonical natural ability, and register the resolved ability so gifts and purchases unlock the same dex entry path. Registered AbilityDex entries keep the canonical ability description separate from game-specific Capture and Field Observation bonuses, which are shown as generated `Partner Effect` rows from the same runtime ability effects used by encounters.

The local Content Studio ability generator at `http://127.0.0.1:3102/abilities` reads the resolved effect list, shows source-data form recommendations where a canonical source ability identifier matches, and can save explicit `effects` back to authored abilities.

Current ability-patch item status:

Ability patches are generated from every authored ability in `ABILITIES` as `{abilityId}-ability-patch` where `{abilityId}` is the ability slug with `_` converted to `-`. Abilities with `locked: true` mark special natural abilities that cannot be overwritten by another ability patch. Patches are no longer authored as item drops or task, battle, expedition, or prize rewards. The Let's Go patch remains available as a paid Viridian City Poké Mart purchase.
Patch learnability is derived from ability metadata: `forms` and canonical `naturalAssignments` control compatible form IDs, while `naturalChance`/assignment `chance` only control natural ability rolls.
Hidden natural assignments are ignored for wild rolls until Ability Insight at Researcher 27. After that unlock, eligible hidden slots roll at 5%, or 10% for Pokemon forms with Pokemon Research level 4+.

| Item                          | Teaches         | Current content status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ----------------------------- | --------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `vital-spirit-ability-patch`  | `vital_spirit`  | Valid ability patch item; no longer awarded by authored drops or rewards.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `sturdy-ability-patch`        | `sturdy`        | Valid ability patch item; no longer awarded by authored drops or rewards.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `water-absorb-ability-patch`  | `water_absorb`  | Valid ability patch item; no longer awarded by authored drops or rewards.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `lightning-rod-ability-patch` | `lightning_rod` | Valid ability patch item; no longer awarded by authored drops or rewards.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `lets-go-ability-patch`       | `lets_go`       | Sold for 1,000 Pokedollars at Viridian City Poké Mart; no longer awarded by the `a-classy-guy` task. Pikachu-only; while it is the active partner, normal catch encounters have a 1-in-32 chance to be replaced by Eevee and a 1-in-512 chance to be replaced by Mimikyu. Key encounters remain protected. |
| `<ability-id>-ability-patch`  | `<ability-id>`  | Defined for all other abilities in `ABILITIES` with standard patch descriptions.                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Location encounters marked `keyEncounter: true` remain protected against encounter-replacement ability effects. The custom Let's Go partner ability now performs the authored Eevee and Mimikyu encounter replacements for Pikachu.

## Moves

Weather-setting moves are now authored as player-usable TMs in `src/data/moves/tms/weather.ts`. Sunny Day, Rain Dance, Sandstorm, Hail, and Snowscape use canonical learner lists; all five are discovered through Castform Research Levels 1–5, while Castform itself is not assigned Snowscape.

Move data is custom and stance-based, not a full canonical Pokemon move list. `MoveConfig.damage` values are multipliers against the shared 50 BP battle baseline, so a 1x move resolves as 50 BP before stance, stats, type, weather, held-item, and other modifiers. `MoveConfig` supports stance, damage multiplier, defender-type damage overrides, weather-only damage multipliers, target, eligible Pokemon forms, explicit excluded forms, forced type, optional type-effectiveness bypass, accuracy, always-hit accuracy bypass, buffs/debuffs, status effects, stance-disable effects, same-turn enemy AI move interruption, reusable attacker-vs-defender contests, move-level gates, secondary statuses, charged moves, recharge moves, continuous moves, delayed damage, and optional enemy AI use timing, terrain-setting effects, and explicit multi-hit metadata. Move configs also support reusable runtime effects for called moves, exact flat/current-HP/user-level/user-current-HP/last-damage-taken damage rules, dynamic typing such as held `{type}-plate` Judgment, held `{type}-memory` Multi-Attack, Drive-based Techno Blast, and weather-shaped Weather Ball, one-use next-damage and next-accuracy effects, conditional damage modifiers including random chance rolls, previous failed move, previous successful move, fainted party member count, user/target positive stat stages, user-no-held-item checks, target Pokemon type checks, user recently took damage, or target Dynamax checks, post-damage status cures, post-damage stat-stage effects such as target-KO boosts, weather-aware healing, stat-stage copy/swap/reset/inversion effects, type-filtered stat-stage boosts, status/party cures including target cures with optional user healing, main-status transfers, secondary-status cures, party revival, forced/pending switches, move locks, move-use depletion, battle conditions including held-item, target-held-item, consumed-held-item, user-status, target-status, first-active-turn, and gender requirements, random, first-known-move, target-primary, last-opponent-move-resistance, set/add/remove battle type changes, player-side battle rewards, held-item transfer/removal/theft/swapping/recycling/self-consumption/berry consumption, and per-side battle item-use availability changes. Move contests can compare level, HP, size, battle stats, effective battle stats, or friendship against the target or an authored number. Move visibility flags include `trainerOnly` for moves wild Pokemon cannot use, `aiOnly` for moves excluded from generated TMs/MoveDex/player assignment, and `manualOnly` for bespoke moves excluded from automatic enemy AI pools unless listed directly on a battle Pokemon; broad unauthored TM libraries now rely on missing/empty `formId` lists rather than `manualOnly`. Buff and debuff stages can include an optional `chance` (defaults to 100) and an optional `target` (defaults to `self` for buffs and `enemy` for debuffs). Player, PVP, and enemy AI move resolution all honor those buff/debuff targets. Main status effects can also include optional `chance` (defaults to 100), `target` (defaults to the move target), and `forceStatus` for moves like Rest that replace an existing main status. `statusTransfer` moves such as Psycho Shift copy the user's current main status to the target through normal status immunity checks, then clear the source status only after a successful transfer. `MoveConfig` also supports `damageRange`, `multiHit`, and `randomStatuses` for bespoke random effects and explicit multi-hit total damage ranges; immediate multi-hit damage resolves a hit count and applies damage in per-hit chunks so hit-sensitive shields, held blocks, Endure, and on-damaged hooks can trigger for each hit. Metronome-style called moves resolve into another authored move before execution, excluding themselves and moves that require follow-up locked turns. Hidden Power is runtime-special-cased instead of using canonical Pokemon formulas: IV parity resolves one of the non-Normal attack types, and average IVs scale its damage multiplier from 0.7x to 1.5x. Advanced and boss enemy AI can generate Hidden Power for any Pokemon form, while normal player assignment and lower enemy AI still respect Hidden Power's authored form list. Secondary statuses are runtime effects separate from normal status; they can target Pokemon, sides, both sides, or the field, trigger before moves, on turn end, or on switch-in, optionally delay before first effect, roll fixed or ranged durations, and apply damage/absorb with optional Pokemon-type filters, healing, regular status, status immunity, wake-status effects, stat stages with optional Pokemon-type filters, outgoing stance/type-specific damage modifiers, incoming stance/type-specific damage reduction or damage-taken modifiers, passive accuracy bypass, type-immunity bypass that makes matching 0x type effectiveness resolve as neutral 1x damage, infatuation, voluntary switch prevention, or move blocking by zero-damage moves, healing moves, repeated last-used moves, explicit move ids, or matching attack types with optional block damage. Reapplying the same secondary status ID to the same Pokemon, side, or field refreshes/replaces it instead of stacking duplicates. Pokemon-attached secondary statuses clear when that Pokemon switches out or when a move authors `secondaryStatusCure`; move-authored cures can target self, enemy, or both, choose Pokemon statuses, side statuses, or both, and optionally clear only named secondary-status IDs. Rapid Spin clears the user's Pokemon and side secondary statuses, while Tidy Up clears authored hazards plus Substitute/Shed Tail decoys from both active sides without removing unrelated effects. Foresight and Odor Sleuth apply an Identified secondary status that lets Normal and Fighting attacks hit Ghost targets neutrally; their canonical evasion reset is intentionally ignored because this game has no evasion stat. Miracle Eye applies the same immunity-bypass pattern for Psychic attacks into Dark targets. Attract applies gender-gated Infatuation to opposite non-genderless targets; infatuated Pokemon have a 50% before-move immobilization roll. Block, Mean Look, Spider Web, and trapping residual moves apply source-linked switch-prevention secondary statuses that block voluntary player, PVP, and trainer AI switching without blocking forced KO replacements or move-forced switches; those trapping effects end when the Pokemon that caused them leaves battle. Camouflage randomly changes the user's active battle type through `typeChangeEffect`; Conversion changes the user's active battle type to its first known battle move's type, Conversion 2 changes the user to a type that resists or is immune to the opponent's last successful authored move or basic stance attack type, and Reflect Type changes the user's active battle type to the target's primary type. Soak and Magic Powder set the target's battle type, Forest's Curse adds Grass, Trick-or-Treat adds Ghost, Smack Down/Thousand Arrows remove Flying, and Roost/Burn Up/Double Shock can temporarily or persistently remove a battle type until duration expiry or switch. Weather Ball is Normal/50 BP in clear weather, changes type from active battle weather, and doubles to 100 BP in non-clear weather. Magnitude uses random damage scaling from 0.2x to 3x. Rototiller creates a five-turn field secondary status that raises Attack and Special Attack when a Grass-type Pokemon switches in. Purify cures the target's main status, fails if no status was cured, and heals the user for half max HP only on a successful cure. Disable now authors `disableStance: { stance: 'random', turns: 2 }`, resolving to one of the target's base stances at use time. Pay Day adds a flat 30 Pokédollar player battle reward through `battleRewards`. Rage is approximated as weak Normal damage that doubles only after the user recently took damage, instead of granting an immediate Attack stage on use. Pledge moves and Fusion Flare/Fusion Bolt can scale from the user's previous successful authored move through a conditional damage modifier. Last Respects scales from fainted allies through `fainted-party-members`, keeping normal typed Ghost damage rather than fixed damage. Nightmare is sleep-gated and applies three turns of 25% max-HP end-turn damage. Curse uses a dedicated branch effect: Ghost users spend half max HP and curse the target for 25% max-HP end-turn damage, while non-Ghost users raise Attack/Defense and lower Speed. Charged moves use `charged` as the number of charge turns before release; charge logs stay generic and hide the move name/stance until release. Vanished charge moves such as Fly, Bounce, Sky Drop, and Phantom Force apply Vanished when charging starts and fail before spending the move if Vanished cannot be applied. Recharge moves use `recharge` as the number of turns after a successful release where the UI is locked to a Recharge action and the player skips attacking while the enemy acts. Continuous moves use `continuous: { min, max }`; after a successful first use, the battle locks the player into that move for the rolled remaining turns, repeating the same effect until the count ends, the move misses/fails, or it is interrupted. Continuous moves can also author `repeatDamage` for per-use damage growth, as Echoed Voice does with +100% per successful repeat up to five uses, and `continuousEnd.status` for effects that apply only when the continuous sequence naturally finishes, as Thrash, Outrage, Petal Dance, and Raging Fury do for self-confusion.
Move contests can compare effective battle stats, raw battle stats, level, current HP, max HP, height, weight, or size, then configure success/failure damage, battle result, failure behavior, and counterattack prevention. Moves can also author `preventCounterOnStanceWin` for stance-win-gated effects such as Sucker Punch without using a stat contest. Stance-fail rules can be authored with `failOnStance`; Protect, Detect, and Quick Guard are one-turn 100% damage-reduction secondary statuses that take hold without stance-loss gating. Specialty guard moves use one-turn 75% stance-specific damage reduction: Baneful Bunker and King's Shield protect against Power, Spiky Shield and Obstruct protect against Speed, and Wide Guard plus Burning Bulwark protect against Tech.

The six stat-contest moves Quick Attack, Slow Strike, Mighty Charge, Accidental Tap, Cunning Trap, and Play Dumb author `contest.failIfCounteredByStance`. Their existing stat comparison must still succeed, and the opponent's stance must not counter the move stance; a tied stance is allowed. This condition is checked for player, enemy, and PVP move resolution, and enemy move scoring avoids selecting a move that the player's stance would counter.

Bind now uses the same source-linked residual-trap model as Wrap and Clamp: its initial hit is authored at the 15 BP-equivalent `0.3` damage multiplier with 85% accuracy, followed by four to five turns of 12.5% max-HP end-turn damage and voluntary switch prevention. It no longer uses a continuous move lock or guaranteed same-turn interruption.

Owned Pokemon must have battle moves assigned from the Pokemon details menu before those moves appear in battle. Each Pokemon stores up to four selected moves in the `pokemon.assignedMoves` Payload field, but Researcher level controls how many slots are usable: 1/2/3/4 slots unlock at Researcher levels 10/20/45/60. The Pokémon details summary renders only those slots; its full-screen move workspace searches and filters the complete eligible library, while Auto-pick stages a balanced loadout through the neutral battle scorer and requires an explicit Save. Move IDs are authored as lowercase move-name slugs, and TM items are generated from those moves as `tm-{move-name}` with a standard description and forced-type sprite. The available assignment pool is built only from owned move-granting TMs/HMs, with form gates still enforced; authored move levels remain for enemy AI and automatic fallback selection, but do not block an explicitly assigned player move. Authored TM/HM move-level values are capped at level 50. Move configs normally use explicit `formId` compatibility, while bespoke moves can also require an already assigned move-name keyword. TM Protective Pollen uses that dynamic gate: a Pokemon must keep a Powder or Spore move assigned alongside it. In battle, the Power Up drawer and server execution use the player's assigned moves regardless of their authored move level, then temporarily fill empty slots with level-aware AI-scored compatible moves from the player's owned TMs/HMs.

Form compatibility is mirrored for regional/form variants that should share the same movepool: Shellos/Gastrodon East Sea forms (`422-east`, `423-east`) follow their West Sea base forms (`422`, `423`), and all typed Arceus forms (`493-{type}`) follow base Arceus (`493`). The `493-unknown` data form is intentionally not included in the typed Arceus move mirror. Alcremie's dessert variants are excluded from generated Pokemon data; only base Alcremie (`869`) and Gigantamax Alcremie (`10223`) remain available.

Owned Pokemon must also select one Pokemon Power before using battle power systems. The selected category is stored in `pokemon.selectedPokemonPower` and can be one of Tera, Mega, Z-Move, Dynamax, Victory Power, Battle Shout, Circadian Power, or Dimensional Shift. The Pokemon details menu only offers currently unlocked/compatible categories for the player's Trainer level; battles filter the shared Powers section to the selected category and server actions repeat the selected-power and Trainer-level checks.

Owned Pokemon can hold one eligible item through `pokemon.heldItemId` once Trainer level 15 unlocks held items. Individual held item definitions do not publish separate Trainer level requirements; the shared held-item unlock governs assignment. Assigning a held item from the Pokemon details menu reserves one copy from inventory; clearing or replacing it returns the reserved copy, and normal held-item battle consumption clears the Pokemon field without returning inventory. Move-caused held item transfer, theft, swapping, throwing, removal, and berry destruction are battle-only item state changes; they can affect the active battle and Recycle, but they do not consume inventory or permanently alter the Pokemon's assigned held item. Trainer Pokemon can also set `heldItemId` on individual `enemyTeam` entries. Light Ball is a Pikachu-only permanent held item that doubles Power- and Tech-stance attack damage while leaving Speed-stance damage unchanged; Pikachu catches and Field Observation appearances have an unconditional independent 1% Light Ball roll. Leek raises Farfetch'd and Sirfetch'd's critical-hit ratio by two stages and drops at 1% from base and Galarian Farfetch'd. Thick Club doubles Cubone and Marowak's Attack and drops at 1% from Cubone, Marowak, and Alolan Marowak. Quick Powder doubles untransformed Ditto's Speed and Metal Powder doubles its Defense; each has a separate independent 1% Ditto drop roll. Current held item support includes all 18 type boost item families in three tiers: diminished tier gives +10% matching-type attack damage with a 3% attack break chance, normal tier gives +10% and never breaks, and Unstable tier gives +15% with a 15% attack break chance; break chances only roll when the item actually boosts that attack's resolved type. Current authored diminished held recipe unlocks are visible recipe prizes: Cotton Scarf from Route 3 catches, Dusty Charcoal from post-shortcut Charmander's Den catches, Magic Water from post-regular Squirtle Cove catches, Weak Magnet from Route 10 catches, Regular Seed from post-trust Secret Garden catches, Poison Tip from Route 22 catches, Brittle Hard Stone from Mt. Moon 1F/B1F/B2F catches, Coarse Sand from Diglett's Cave catches, Fairy Down from Clefairy Cavern catches, Rusty Coat from Rock Tunnel metal mining, and Aluminium Powder from Buggy Champion. The other seven diminished held recipe manuals remain secret placeholders until their prize sources are authored. Metal Coat remains evolution-only; Steel's normal held boost item is Metal Polish. Training Bands cover HP, Attack, Defense, Sp. Atk, Sp. Def, and Speed; each lowers its matching battle stat by 15% while held, then silently grants 1 matching EV up to the 252-per-stat/510-total caps on battle wins with a 2% held-item consumption roll. The generated Pokemon form data also carries each form's mainline source EV yield, and winning PVE battles awards the yield from every defeated enemy to each involved owned Pokemon inside the atomic result transaction; PVP does not award EVs. All 18 Memories are authored as held battle items that change Multi-Attack's type when held, using the matching TM type sprite. Burn, Douse, Shock, and Chill Drives are authored as held battle items that change Techno Blast's type and use dedicated Drive sprites. Oran/Sitrus HP berries trigger at 50% HP or less, and status berries Cheri, Chesto, Pecha, Rawst, Aspear, Persim, and Lum trigger on matching status. Held items work in PVE and PVP.

Pokemon now persist `itemCharge` for held items that build energy during battle. Removing or replacing a held item resets that Pokemon's charge to 0. Inferior Fire, Water, Leaf, Thunder, Shiny, and Ice Stones are held charge items: they gain 5% charge when the holder is hit by the matching attack type, with Shiny using Fairy attacks. At 100% charge the held Inferior Stone is removed from the Pokemon, charge resets to 0, and the owner receives the matching evolution stone. Inferior Light and Dark Stones instead gain 2% charge from any damaging attack during their active windows: Light from 06:00-17:59 and Dark from 18:00-05:59. Light completes into Dawn Stone from 06:00-06:59 and Sun Stone otherwise; Dark completes into Dusk Stone from 18:00-18:59 and Moon Stone otherwise.

Electric move data now also includes unauthored TM configs for the broader canonical Electric move family, including Charge Beam, Electroweb, Shock Wave, Spark, Thunder Fang, Parabolic Charge, Volt Switch, Thunderclap, Rising Voltage, Thunder Punch, Zing Zap, Thunder Cage, Overdrive, Discharge, Bolt Beak, Wild Charge, Buzzy Buzz, Wildbolt Storm, Supercell Slam, Plasma Fists, Fusion Bolt, Electro Drift, Thunder, Aura Wheel, Zap Cannon, Volt Tackle, Double Shock, Electro Shot, Bolt Strike, Electro Ball, Pika Papow, Charge, Magnetic Flux, Ion Deluge, and Electrify. Volt Switch uses the same pending self-switch support as U-turn and Flip Turn, and Fusion Bolt scales after Fusion Flare. These are authored without reward locations or form assignment lists for now. Terrain moves are intentionally not authored. Z-Move, Dynamax, and Gigantamax attacks are intentionally not authored as TM/type moves because those mechanics are handled through Pokemon Powers.

Normal move data now includes a broader unauthored TM config library for canonical Normal attacks and status moves beyond the current rewarded set. Newly supported Normal effects include Metronome/Assist/Nature Power random authored calls, Copycat/Mimic last-move calls, Snore/Sleep Talk sleep-gated use, Sonic Boom fixed damage, Super Fang current-HP damage, Endeavor HP matching, Pain Split HP averaging, Judgment held-plate typing, Multi-Attack held-memory typing, Techno Blast Drive typing, Facade/Smelling Salts conditional damage, Chip Away defender-stat-stage bypass, Lock-On/Mind Reader next-accuracy bypass, Encore move locks, Blood Moon repeat-use blocking, Morning Sun weather-aware healing, Safeguard/Uproar status prevention, Uproar wake effects, Wish delayed healing, Bestow held-item transfer, Covet held-item theft, Recycle held-item restoration, Teatime held-berry consumption, Psych Up stat copying, Power Shift stat-stage swapping, Transform battle copying, Reflect Type target-primary typing, Revelation Dance/Raging Bull user-primary typing, Raging Bull screen/status cleanup, Heal Bell/Refresh cures, Revival Blessing party revival, Roar/Whirlwind forced switching, Baton Pass/Shed Tail pending switching, False Swipe/Hold Back one-use +60% next-damage boosts, Yawn delayed sleep, Attract gender-gated infatuation, Captivate gender-gated Sp. Atk drops, Block/Mean Look/Wrap-style switch prevention, and Stockpile/Swallow/Spit Up battle item-use availability manipulation. Sketch is implemented as a Smeargle-only Research Level 1 TM: a successful battle use has a Research Level-scaled chance to capture one player-usable move from the opposing active Pokémon (25% at Level 1, 30% at Level 2, 35% at Level 3, 40% at Level 4, and 50% at Level 5), with the capture committed only after a battle win. Recorded moves bypass TM ownership for Smeargle and appear in the MoveDex Sketched view once Smeargle reaches Research Level 1. These new Normal move configs currently have no reward locations or Pokemon form assignment lists.

Fire move data now also includes a broader unauthored TM config library for canonical Fire attacks and status moves beyond Ember, Flame Charge, and Fire Spin. The new data covers burn chances, drain, recoil, post-use recharge attacks, continuous attacks, critical-hit boosts, trapping residual damage and switch prevention for Fire Spin/Magma Storm, weight contests, stat boosts/debuffs, defensive shields, and held-berry consumption for Incinerate. Weather-setting moves such as Sunny Day are intentionally not authored here because battle weather is handled by the separate Weather Control power system. Z-Move, Dynamax, and Gigantamax attacks are intentionally not authored as TM/type moves because those mechanics are handled through Pokemon Powers.

Water move data now also includes a broader unauthored TM config library for canonical Water attacks and status moves beyond the existing Water first-partner and HM-style entries. The new data covers priority-style Speed contests, healing rings, drain, recoil, recharge, vanish-style Dive, pending self-switching, trapping residual damage, switch-in chip, critical-hit moves, burn/paralysis/confusion chances, Sparkling Aria post-damage burn curing, stat boosts/debuffs, Fire damage reduction from Water Sport, and current-system approximations for accuracy drops, Soak, Brine, and HP-scaling attacks. Weather-setting moves such as Rain Dance are intentionally not authored here because battle weather is handled by the separate Weather Control power system. Z-Move, Dynamax, and Gigantamax attacks are intentionally not authored as TM/type moves because those mechanics are handled through Pokemon Powers.

The remaining canonical TM move table in `movestoauthor.txt` has been imported as broad TM config libraries for Grass, Ice, Fighting, Poison, Ground, Flying, Psychic, Bug, Rock, Ghost, Dragon, Dark, Steel, and Fairy. Their Pokemon compatibility can be bulk-authored with `bun run moves:assign-recommended-forms`, which applies the same source-data recommendations as the Move Editor's per-move "Apply recommended forms" action while excluding Normal moves. These entries still need explicit future reward/unlock locations before players can broadly obtain their TMs. Their effects use supported primitives: damage multipliers from listed base power, status chances, stat stages, drains, recoil, pre-use charge/post-use recharge/continuous turns, priority-style Speed contests, forced/pending switches, fixed/OHKO/user-level/user-current-HP/last-damage-taken/party-count damage rules, delayed future damage, residual trap/switch chip, secondary-status cleanup, held-item effects, random-chance, previous-move, user-no-held-item and target-type checks, and target-Dynamax conditional damage, defender stat-stage bypass, user/target stat-stage, post-damage target-KO stat stages, user-recently-damaged, same-turn stat-lowered, target-status gates, consumed-held-item gates, and target-switching conditional damage, target-sourced attack stats, battle type set/add/remove effects, stat-stage reset/swap/inversion, damage reduction/modifier and accuracy-bypass secondary statuses, move-blocking, Snatch, terrain-setting move effects, and faint-trigger secondary statuses, and adapted single-battle versions of ally/contact/stance mechanics. The Steel TM library has been audited against description-only effects: Anchor Shot traps with switch prevention; Autotomize targets the user; Doom Desire queues a delayed Steel hit; Double Iron Bash and Iron Head use interrupt chances for flinch; Gear Up, Shift Gear, Spin Out, and Steel Beam apply their described self effects; Gyro Ball and Heavy Slam use speed/weight contests; Make It Rain adds the flat player battle reward; Metal Burst returns 1.5x the user's most recent received damage; and Tachyon Cutter is authored as two guaranteed hits. The non-Fire/Steel/Dark/Fairy remaining type files have also been audited for description-only gaps: Ingrain now grants Regen and switch prevention, while Sand Tomb, Thousand Waves, Spirit Shackle, No Retreat, and Octolock apply switch prevention where described; Mortal Spin clears hazards/trapping effects and poisons instead of applying trap damage; Toxic Thread, Venom Drench, Breaking Swipe, Lumina Crash, Take Heart, Coaching, No Retreat, Clangorous Soul, Scale Shot, Ancient Power, Silver Wind, Ominous Wind, Meteor Beam, Order Up, Tailwind, and Glitzy Glow now expose their stat or shielding effects; Counter, Oblivion Wing, Matcha Gotcha, Heal Order, Pollen Puff, Spectral Thief, Power Trick, Teleport, Dragon Darts, Dual Wingbeat, Fickle Beam, Prismatic Laser, Eternabeam, Heal Block, Psychic Noise, Glaive Rush, Guard Split, Power Split, Psyshock, Psystrike, and Secret Sword now use matching runtime primitives or explicit single-battle adaptations. Psychic/Rock/Flying/Poison fixes now include Eerie Spell move-use depletion, Future Sight delayed damage, partial Guard Swap/Power Swap stat-stage swaps, flinch interrupts for Extrasensory/Heart Stamp/Zen Headbutt/Air Slash/Floaty Fall/Sky Attack, Stored Power stat-stage scaling, Aerial Ace always-hit authoring, Acrobatics no-held-item scaling, Fly's Vanished charge turn, Bounce's Vanished charge turn, Sky Drop's Vanished charge turn, Pluck berry consumption, Rollout repeat scaling, Salt Cure residual type bonus, Tar Shot Fire damage-taken vulnerability, Belch's consumed-held-item gate, Coil's Defense and accuracy adaptation, and Venoshock poisoned-target scaling. Bug move fixes now include Fell Stinger's post-KO Attack boost and Powder blocking and damaging Fire-type attacks. Ghost move fixes now include Last Respects fainted-ally scaling, Curse's Ghost/non-Ghost branch, Grudge's faint-triggered move-use depletion and Spite move-use depletion, Hex and Infernal Parade status scaling, Nightmare sleep-gated residual damage, Phantom Force's Vanished charge turn, Poltergeist held-item gating, Rage Fist recent-damage scaling, Shadow Punch always-hit authoring, and Destiny Bond's faint-bond secondary status. The Dark TM library has been audited against current engine support: Baddy Bad applies a Power-stance damage reduction, Beat Up scales from conscious party count, Bite/Dark Pulse/Fiery Wrath use interrupt chances for flinch, Comeuppance returns recent received damage, Flatter confuses and raises the target's Sp. Atk, Fling requires and consumes the user's held item, Foul Play uses the target's attack stat, Jaw Lock traps both active Pokemon, Lash Out doubles after same-turn stat drops, Memento and Parting Shot apply both listed stat drops, Obstruct adds the adapted defense drop, Payback/Power Trip/Punishment/Pursuit use conditional damage, Quash interrupts the target's same-turn action, Ruination halves current HP, Snatch steals supported beneficial self effects, Taunt blocks zero-damage moves, Throat Chop blocks authored sound move IDs, Torment blocks repeating the last successful move, and Wicked Torque uses a sleep chance. Remaining notes are intentional authoring comments for authored multi-hit damage ranges, battle-flow adaptations such as Pursuit using side-level switch intent, or flavor-only item text. Weather-setting entries are intentionally not authored because battle weather is controlled through the Weather Control power system. Electric Terrain, Grassy Terrain, Misty Terrain, and Psychic Terrain are authored as future TM moves with no learner or reward lists yet. Z-Move, Dynamax, and Gigantamax attacks from that source table are also intentionally not authored as TM/type moves because those mechanics are handled through Pokemon Powers.

Status effects used by moves and battle logic include paralysis, sleep, poison, bad poison, burn, freeze, frostbite, confusion, veil, regeneration, mystic veil, shield tiers, T-shield tiers, S-shield tiers, victory, and vanished. Stance-disable effects are stored separately from normal status so they can coexist with status conditions. Vanished now blocks incoming damage while active, then clears at the next selected attack and grants a 1.3x damage bonus on that attack. Regeneration heals 1/8 max HP at turn end. Bad poison ramps its end-turn damage, sleep blocks movement for a hidden 2-4 turn duration before waking, freeze blocks movement until a thaw check succeeds, confusion can make a Pokemon hurt itself before acting, and sunlight prevents new freeze applications.

Battle weather uses the encounter's saved weather snapshot. The shared damage engine applies Fire/Water weather multipliers, primal weather attack blocking, sandstorm Rock Sp. Def, snow Ice Defense, strong-winds Flying weakness mitigation, fog accuracy reduction for non-perfect-accuracy moves, and end-turn sandstorm/hail/snowstorm/shadowy-aura chip damage.

Seismic Toss uses the user-level damage rule and therefore deals damage equal to the user's level. Flower Trick, Frost Breath, Storm Throw, Surging Strikes, Wicked Blow, and Zippy Zap use deterministic 100% critical-hit authoring, while Battle Armor and Shell Armor retain their intended critical-hit immunity. Zippy Zap is restricted to Pikachu forms, including cap and Gigantamax forms.

## Special Pokemon Flags

Special Pokemon flags are stored on Pokemon records and can be authored on Pokemon rewards and battle enemies.

| ----------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `isShadow`  | Pokemon collection, Pokemon reward `pokemonData`, battle enemy config                                                         | +10% Attack and Special Attack, shadow visual aura, takes 1/8 max HP self-damage at turn end after attacking, cannot use battle powers. Switching or using an item does not trigger the damage. | Existing Team Rocket content uses Shadow enemies and Rocket Ball captures create Shadow Pokemon. |

Attacks have a 10% chance to deal 1.1x damage and log `{pokemon}'s aura burns bright.`                                                                | Mechanic is implemented and reserved for future authored content.                                |

## Field TMs

Field TMs/HMs are inventory items generated in `src/data/items/entries/tms.ts`. The five field HMs retain their legacy `tm-*` IDs for stored-inventory and reward compatibility, but display as HM01 through HM05 instead of as TMs. All current Field TMs/HMs link to assignable battle moves through `moveId`; reward authoring still controls which ones players can naturally obtain.

| Field TM                      | Current status                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `tm-cut` / HM01 Cut           | Rewarded by the one-time `ss-anne-repair-duty` expedition after the Pikachu power-on task. Used as a gate for Viridian, Pewter, Vermilion Gym, and Route 9 shrub tasks. Also unlocks the assignable Cut battle move for forms 5, 6, 28, 31, 34, 47, 83, 123, and 141. |
| `tm-fly` / HM02 Fly           | Secret reward from Celadon's `nesting-season` task after delivering 20 Nest Ball Pidgey and 20 Nest Ball Spearow to the Birdkeeper. Unlocks the assignable Fly battle move for forms 6, 18, 22, 142, 144, 145, and 146 if owned.                                      |
| `tm-surf` / HM03 Surf         | Defined, but no current player-facing task gate or authored reward. Unlocks the assignable Surf battle move for forms 9, 73, 130, and 131 if owned.                                                                         |
| `tm-strength` / HM04 Strength | One-time first-completion reward from the `safari-zone-grand-expedition`. Unlocks the assignable Strength battle move for forms 9, 31, 34, 67, 68, 76, and 112 if owned.                                                                                                                                   |
| `tm-flash` / HM05 Flash       | Rewarded by `a-light-in-the-dark`; unlocks the assignable Flash battle move for forms 25, 26, 81, 82, 100, 101, 120, 121, 125, 135, and 145.                                                                                                                          |

Other notable progression key items include `unknown-tm`, a unique blue Water TM decoy from Vermilion fishing; `squirtle-squad`, a unique diary key item from the 1% Squirtle random event gated by `unknown-tm`; `clear-bell`, a unique key item that gates the Ho-Oh and Legendary Beast random Snap/Battle/Field Observation loops; `rainbow-feather`, a non-unique key item from the Clear Bell-gated 1:256 Ho-Oh shadow Snap random event capped at three successful snaps; `silver-feather`, a non-unique key item from the ungated 1:256 Lugia shadow Snap counterpart capped at three successful snaps; `proof-of-fire`, `proof-of-thunder`, and `proof-of-water`, proof key items from the one-time successful 1:256 Legendary Beast shadow Snap random events; the Lifeless/Concentrated Fire, Thunder, and Water key item chain from beast-stone fusion tasks and Artisan recipes; and the Flaming/Charged/Frozen Branch, Bough, Twig, Lifeless Branch, and Concentrated Branch key item chain from the matching Legendary Bird random events, branch-fusion tasks, and Silver Feather Artisan recipes.

## Battle Powers

Power data lives in `src/data/powers.ts`; item definitions live in key items and battle items. Power usage requires both the normal key item/item requirements and the relevant Trainer level. Power counters default to one use per battle, and battle configs can set explicit hard caps when content needs to lower that. The engine supports more than current progression exposes.

All activations also spend three successful stance-matchup wins from a shared PvE-team pool or a per-participant PvP pool; each winning doubles lane can add one. Turn-count gates and separate Dimensional Shift win/loss/draw charges are no longer activation requirements. Z-Move arming is immediate and free of turn cost, but still spends the three-win pool and its normal one-use counter. The next successful basic stance attack has 250 base power; a Dynamax basic stance has 100 base power with `MAX` naming. The full power set is available in PvE and PvP singles/doubles subject to each power's normal item, assignment, and level requirements. Existing key items and authored reward locations are unchanged.

Battle level syncing uses the highest rolled opposing level. Ordinary trainer battles sync the player to five levels above that value, while ordinary wild battles use ten levels above it; both are bounded by the player's persistent badge level cap. Gyms, bosses, tutorials, chronicles, PvP, special events, and other deliberate set pieces can retain an explicit `levelCap` override. VS Seeker uses the level selected by the player as its explicit cap.

`vs-seeker` is rewarded by the one-time `pokemon-fan-club-expedition`. It is a reusable key item that appears at the top of Explore when ready and starts a dynamic 3v3 trainer battle every 30 minutes. The player chooses a level from 20 through the Pokemon badge cap in five-level steps and a five-step difficulty. Teams sample non-legendary, non-mythical enemy Pokemon from the player's seen Pokedex forms; higher difficulty weights higher base-stat-total forms more heavily while keeping the selection random, and also improves the random trainer IV and EV rolls. Each opposing Pokemon has a 30% chance to hold an eligible berry, and the trainer has a 50% chance to bring one healing item. VS Seeker uses the `trainer` AI profile until all 8 Kanto badges are owned, then uses `advanced`. Rewards pay 350 PokeDollars plus 150 for each five-level step above level 20, multiplied by 1x-3x difficulty, plus 1-5 League Tickets matching difficulty, one level-matched Candy, and 5 level-matched PokePowder. Trainer healing item rolls start with Potion, add Super Potion after 4 Kanto badges, add Hyper Potion after 6, and add Full Restore after all 8.

Implemented power systems:

| System             | Key item or item                              | Trainer level | Engine status                                                                                                                                                               | Current content status                                                                                    |
| ------------------ | --------------------------------------------- | ------------: | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Tera               | `tera-orb` plus `tera-shard-*` Pokemon items  |            70 | Supported; Tera Shards set an owned Pokemon's stored Tera type outside battle, then the Tera Orb changes the active Pokemon into that type for 3 turns.                     | No key item or shards are currently rewarded/sold.                                                        |
| Mega Evolution     | `mega-bracelet` plus matching Mega Stone      |            75 | Supported; 93 Mega/Primal mappings, lasts for the rest of battle after use.                                                                                                 | Mega Stones are generated, but no `mega-bracelet` or Mega Stones are currently authored into progression. |
| Z-Move             | `z-ring`                                      |            80 | Supported; arms without spending a turn and empowers the next successful basic stance attack to 250 base power. The charge survives other actions and misses. | `z-ring` is defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                     |
| Dynamax/Gigantamax | `dynamax-band`                                |            85 | Supported; 32 Gigantamax mappings, lasts 3 turns, increases current/max HP by 1.5x without changing other stats, and makes basic stances 100 base power. | `dynamax-band` is defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                               |
| Victory Power      | `v-crown`                                     |            90 | State and status effect exist.                                                                                                                                              | Key item is defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                     |
| Shouts             | `book-of-shouts`                              |            35 | PVE activation consumes the player's action, gives the enemy a normal response, and raises the active Pokemon's Attack, Defense, Sp. Atk, Sp. Def, and Speed by one stage for three turns. It cannot stack and clears on switch, faint, or stat reset. | Key item is defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                     |
| Weather Power      | `weather-orb`                                     |            65 | Supported in PvE/PvP singles/doubles; performs a special action based on current weather and does not consume Weather Cores. | Weather Orb is defined; weather cores remain legacy data for compatibility. |
| Circadian Power    | `circadian-stone`                             |            95 | Battle config counters exist as `circadianUses` / `circadianUsesRemaining`.                                                                                                 | Key item is defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                     |
| Dimensional Shift  | `griseous-orb`, `adamant-orb`, `lustrous-orb` |            95 | Time/space/chaos effects use the shared three-win activation cost; legacy win/loss/draw counters are no longer gates. | Key items are defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                   |
| Primal Reversion   | `blue-orb`, `red-orb`                         |            75 | Represented through Mega evolution mappings for Kyogre/Groudon.                                                                                                             | Key items are defined, but not currently rewarded/sold.                                                   |

Most current authored battles do not set battle-specific item, move, or power caps. `itemsPerBattle`, `movesPerBattle`, and the individual power counter fields remain available on battle config as hard caps against the normal Trainer-skill or one-use defaults. Player and PVE enemy move-use limits are applied per Pokemon at battle start. PVE enemy AI move uses default from the highest enemy Pokemon level using the same battle-move ladder as Trainer level; `enemyMovesPerBattle` remains available as an explicit per-enemy-Pokemon special-battle override.

PVE trainer battle configs can also author `trainerItems`. Each entry references an item and optional `quantity` defaulting to 1. Enemy AI scores configured trainer items from their battle effects, currently healing, status clearing, and battle-stat buffs; powers such as Tera and Z-Move remain player-only for enemy AI. Trainers can use at most one configured item per turn. Moves that change item-use availability interact with these assigned quantities for enemies and with current battle item availability for players, without changing the configured max item capacity. PVE battle enemies select up to four AI move IDs at battle start from compatible authored moves plus any explicit `enemyTeam[].aiMoves` manual assignments. Manual assignments are retained in the loadout. Generated loadouts score only a Pokemon's own move properties and do not inspect the opposing team. Broad zero-damage opponent status/debuff moves are excluded from wild loadouts and can fill only one generated slot at a 20%/45%/70% chance for trainer/advanced/boss profiles, respectively; `aiUse` moves and explicit assignments bypass that pacing. This preserves variety without making Toxic, Hypnosis, or similar moves universal. Per-turn decisions use one unified scorer across AI moves, items, and trainer switches, with move scoring based on damage, accuracy, type/weather effectiveness, healing, absorbs, buffs/debuffs, status effects, secondary statuses, self-damage, interrupt potential, and remaining move budget. Authored enemy moves roll their configured accuracy when executed in retaliation or enemy-only turns; misses skip damage, move effects, on-hit held effects, successful move history, and follow-up locks. Enemy-authored moves support battle conditions, effective-Speed contests, stance-disable effects, delayed damage queues, charged turns, recharge turns, continuous move locks, and self-pivot switches; `battleRewards` remain player-only. Normal basic stance attacks still roll from the original weighted stance percentages instead of countering the player's submitted stance, and move selection ignores that hidden current-turn stance before final stance multipliers resolve. Forced enemy replacements after a KO use the same matchup scorer without spending a turn, so trainers select the best available bench Pokemon by offense, incoming type risk, and HP instead of only checking broad type buckets. Damaging moves are hard-skipped when their resolved attack type would have no effect. Protect, Detect, and comparable defensive secondary-status moves apply before the opposing move's damage is resolved in both PVE and PVP, then expire at turn end.

Dusk Mane and Dawn Wings Necrozma Ultra Burst through the Z-Move system: once a prepared Z-Move is actually used, the active fused Necrozma changes to Ultra Necrozma for the rest of the battle with Ultra Necrozma stats and typing. The stored owned Pokemon form is unchanged.

Player battle item use checks item `skillRequirements` before consumption. Trainer level controls item capacity per battle: 2 at level 1, 3 at 17, 4 at 38, and 5 at 52. Trainer level also controls trained move uses per Pokemon per battle: 2 at level 10, 3 at 21, 4 at 42, and 5 at 59. Current battle-use gates are Trainer level 1 for Battle Observer, Trainer level 3 for Potion, Trainer level 4 for Fresh Water, Trainer levels 5-9 for Antidote, Paralyze Heal, Awakening, Burn Heal, and Ice Heal, Trainer level 27 for Super Potion, Trainer level 29 for Soda Pop, Trainer level 34 for Lemonade, Trainer level 32 for all X items, Trainer level 36 for Dire Hit, Trainer level 37 for Full Heal, Trainer level 40 for Training Bands, Trainer level 51 for Hyper Potion, Trainer level 60 for Max Potion, and Trainer level 81 for Full Restore. Held berries use Trainer item gates when assigned, including Trainer level 41 for Sitrus Berry. Berries cannot be used directly from inventory, Pokemon item dialogs, or battle item selectors; color/utility berries act as crafting materials, while eligible Oran/Sitrus/status berries act only through held-item systems. Pokemon-targeted items can also author reusable form-change effects, currently used by Rusty Sword, Rusty Shield, Griseous Orb, Adamant Orb, Lustrous Orb, Rotom form manuals, Ogerpon masks, Reveal Glass, Gracidea, Prison Bottle, and Meteorite. Pokemon-targeted fusion effects are used by DNA Splicers, N-Solarizer, N-Lunarizer, and Reins of Unity; they fuse Kyurem/Necrozma/Calyrex with the selected owned partner, hide that partner from normal availability, and unfuse both records when the same item is used on the fused Pokemon. Held item assignment syncs Plate, Memory, and Drive form families for Arceus, Silvally, and Genesect. Assigned move changes also sync Keldeo between base and Resolute Form based on whether Sacred Sword is assigned, and Meloetta between base and Pirouette Form based on whether Relic Song is assigned. No berry grants direct friendship. Ball usage is not skill-gated; ball access is controlled by shops, drops, Artisan recipes, materials, and recipe/manual unlocks. Lures and repels are Explorer-gated outside battle, with Repel at 20, Super Repel at 40, and Max Repel at 75.

Owned Pokemon lose 1 friendship, clamped at 0, whenever they faint in PVE or PVP battles. NPC trainer and wild enemy Pokemon do not persist friendship changes.

## TCG Battle Pokémon Ability Update

Board-wide Weakness removal is also resolved for the target's side when a live provider's passive ability grants it to all, Basic, Stage, Restored, or Colorless Pokémon in play. The Uxie/Azelf gate requires both named Pokémon on that side's board. Abilities gated by attached Energy or other untracked board conditions are skipped.

Passive retreat locks now block retreat when an opposing live Ability provider restricts the Active Pokémon or restricts Pokémon with the target's matching Special Condition. Active-provider and provider-status requirements are honored. The ability audit recognizes 38 retreat rules across 29 sets, including retreat-cost modifiers and locks.

Weakness resolution now supports board-wide ×2/×3/×4 multipliers, Volbeat and Mesprit/Azelf requirements, opponent Active Pokémon ×4 effects, Colorless-to-Psychic Weakness changes, and Psychic/Darkness Weakness exceptions when the required board and target scopes are represented. The audit recognizes 47 Weakness-rule texts across 35 sets; conditions requiring Stadiums, attached Energy, or unrepresented card zones are skipped.

Resistance-ignore Abilities now support attacker-specific, team/type, Single Strike, and named Pokémon scopes when the board conditions can be verified. The Lunatone partner gate is checked on the attacker's side. Five matching ability records appear across five sets.

Conditional attack damage bonuses also check equal hand sizes across both sides and count opposing Pokémon-GX and Pokémon-ex for per-card bonus formulas.

Reactive passive abilities can now inflict their supported status or damage-counter effect on the attacking Active Pokémon after an opposing attack damages the holder. Active/evolved-holder, knockout, named-team-count, and Special Condition requirements are enforced. Coin-flip retaliation and attached-Energy formulas remain unsupported. The set audit recognizes 71 matching retaliation records across 49 sets.

Team-wide passive Special Condition immunities are checked against the provider side's live board for all-team and supported type/subtype scopes. Team abilities that explicitly recover eligible Pokémon from existing conditions clear those conditions before checkup damage.

Passive between-turn counter damage now supports board scopes for active, benched, asleep/poisoned, Basic, Pokémon-ex/LV.X, and Pokémon with Powers/Bodies targets. Provider activity, status, target exclusions, and non-stacking clauses are checked; attached-Energy and Stadium requirements are skipped. Nineteen matching records appear across 14 sets.
Activated status Powers now support all five Special Conditions on the opponent's Active Pokémon, extending the previous Poison/Burn handling to Asleep, Confused, and Paralyzed when the printed once-per-turn Active-provider clause matches. The set audit recognizes 8 matching records across 4 sets.
Direct once-per-turn draw Powers now support fixed counts of one or more cards from the Pokémon-only deck, including source Active Spot conditions. Composite effects such as energy attachment plus draw, hand/deck shuffles, conditional bonuses, and end-turn choices remain separate patterns. The audit recognizes 21 direct draw Powers across 15 sets; 829 records currently match at least one implemented family, while 3,725 remain unmatched.
Draw-to-hand-size Powers now parse the printed hand target, supporting sizes beyond three. The audit recognizes 10 such Powers across 7 sets; 833 records match at least one implemented family and 3,721 remain unmatched.
Activated healing Powers now support fixed healing of the Active Pokémon and fixed healing across all eligible friendly Pokémon, including supported type scopes. Healing is capped at each Pokémon's missing HP. The audit recognizes 29 activated healing Powers across 18 sets; 850 records match at least one implemented family and 3,704 remain unmatched.
Simple once-per-turn “each player draws a card” Powers now draw one card for each side from its Pokémon-only deck. The audit recognizes 23 activated draw Powers across 17 sets; overall 852 records match an implemented family and 3,702 remain unmatched.
Fixed healing Powers may now target one chosen friendly Pokémon from the front or bench, with supported type filters, alongside Active-only and all-eligible-Pokémon scopes. Healing is capped at missing HP. The audit recognizes 34 activated healing Powers across 23 sets; 857 records match at least one implemented family and 3,697 remain unmatched.
Healing Powers expressed as damage-counter removal convert each counter to 10 HP. Supported “only one named Power per turn” and source Special Condition gates are enforced. The set audit now recognizes 40 activated healing Powers across 29 sets; 863 records match an implemented family and 3,691 remain unmatched.
Bench-to-Active switch Powers accept self-named provider conditions and verify the name against the actual Pokémon card before using the existing swap action. The audit recognizes 35 switch Powers across 18 sets; overall 865 records match an implemented family and 3,689 remain unmatched.
Passive opponent Retreat Cost increases now add their Colorless amount to the opposing Active Pokémon's retreat payment for supported Active-target, opponent-board, and status-specific scopes. Ability provider activity and Basic/Evolved target gates are checked. The UI and server compute retreat cost through the same utility. The audit recognizes 50 retreat rules across 37 sets; 877 records match an implemented family and 3,677 remain unmatched.
Friendly passive Retreat Cost effects now cover supported type and subtype scopes for fixed reductions and no-cost rules, while continuing to omit Energy-, Stadium-, Tool-, and turn-dependent clauses. The generated set audit recognizes 52 retreat rules across 39 sets; 879 records match an implemented family and 3,675 remain unmatched.
Draw Powers can check the saved prior-opponent-turn knockout record, apply once-per-turn limits shared across same-named copies, and enforce source Special Condition clauses. The audit recognizes 40 activated draw Powers across 25 sets; 896 records match an implemented family and 3,658 remain unmatched.
Coin-flip passive protection now supports an on-head fixed damage reduction in addition to preventing all damage. It also supports saving the holder from attack damage that would Knock it Out, leaving 10 HP on heads. Reduction applies after Weakness and Resistance.
